1: //! Stage 2.3(a): the whole-region DOF ladder.
2: //!
3: //! Try to explain each smooth region (stage 2.2) with ONE surface, ascending
4: //! degrees of freedom; the first fit that validates wins. Simplest-first
5: //! resolves degeneracy (any plane also lies on an infinite-radius cylinder; a
6: //! single facet is exactly a plane) without coverage heuristics.
7: //!
8: //! | Rung | Surface  | DOF |
9: //! |------|----------|-----|
10: //! | 1    | Plane    | 3   |
11: //! | 2    | Sphere   | 4   |
12: //! | 3    | Conical  | 5/6 | (cylinder-or-cone; the fitter prefers cylinder)
13: //! | 4    | Torus    | 7   |
14: //!
15: //! The conical rung fits a cylinder first and only falls to a cone when the
16: //! cylinder does not validate (`ConicalSurface::fit_surface`), so it covers
17: //! both the 5-DOF cylinder and 6-DOF cone cases in DOF order.
18: //!
19: //! **Jet-informed order between the sphere and conical rungs.** Strict
20: //! simplest-first breaks on a short cylinder/cone tessellated as a few rings:
21: //! two same-radius rings at heights ±h lie exactly on a sphere of radius
22: //! √(R²+h²), so the lower-DOF sphere would validate and wrongly win. The jet
23: //! resolves it — a developable region (median `min/max` principal-curvature
24: //! ratio ~0) tries the conical rung before the sphere rung; a bi-curved
25: //! region (ratio ~1) keeps sphere-first. The jet only ORDERS; a genuine
26: //! sphere it misreads still falls through to the sphere rung, since a
27: //! cylinder fit cannot validate a sphere's vertices (`region_is_developable`).
28: //!
29: //! **Validation decides all geometry** (Design Principle #1): every claimed
30: //! vertex within `--vertex-tolerance`, every claimed face centroid within
31: //! `--surface-tolerance`, and orientation coherence (every face normal
32: //! agrees in sign with the surface normal at its centroid — the per-type
33: //! `convexity_check`, which also fixes the hypothesis `convex` flag). The
34: //! fitters apply their own numeric-validity guards (cone half-angle bounds,
35: //! torus `0 < r ≤ R`, sphere radius cap) and return `None` outside them.
36: //!
37: //! A region the ladder cannot explain with one surface is a tangent chain
38: //! (pill capsule, rounded-cube shell, pipe-elbow tube); it goes to stage
39: //! 2.3(b)/(c) scoped competition (`compete.rs`). Whatever competition leaves
40: //! unclaimed **terminally degrades to per-facet planes** — total coverage
41: //! can never fail, and a single triangle is exactly a plane.
42: 
43: use crate::config::Config;
44: use crate::stage1::ConnectedMesh;
45: 
46: use super::surface::SurfaceType;
47: use super::bspline::{self, BSplineHypothesis};
48: use super::compete::{self, CompeteHyp};
49: use super::conical::ConicalSurface;
50: use super::edges::JetField;
51: use super::planar::{fit_plane, PlanarSurface};
52: use super::regions::Region;
53: use super::spherical::SphericalSurface;
54: use super::toroidal::ToroidalSurface;
55: use super::{
56:     face_centroid, ConicalHypothesis, PlanarHypothesis, SelectedSurface, SphericalHypothesis,
57:     ToroidalHypothesis, MIN_CYLINDER_FACES, MIN_SPHERE_FACES, MIN_TORUS_FACES,
58: };
59: 
60: use std::collections::HashSet;
61: 
62: /// Regions larger than this many faces are fitted on a deterministic
63: /// stride subsample (validated against the whole region, refit-augmented on
64: /// a near-miss). Bounds fit cost on huge regions — which, lacking scoped
65: /// competition, are the tangent chains that fail the ladder anyway.
66: const MAX_FIT_FACES: usize = 3000;
67: 
68: /// Bounded refit rounds: after a subsample fit, the worst validation
69: /// offenders are added to the fit set and the surface is refit, so a large
70: /// clean surface still validates end to end.
71: const MAX_REFIT: usize = 3;
72: 
73: /// How many worst-offender faces to fold into the fit set per refit round.
74: const REFIT_ADD: usize = 32;
75: 
76: /// Give-up threshold for a refit near-miss: if the worst offender exceeds
77: /// this multiple of `--surface-tolerance`, the region is decisively not this
78: /// surface and no amount of augmenting will help.
79: const REFIT_GIVE_UP: f64 = 8.0;
80: 
81: // The former `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX` whole-region discard lived here. It is gone;
82: // `dissolve_fragmented_surfaces` replaces it. See DEVELOPMENT_PLAN,
83: // "The leftover blob is a boundary problem, not a size problem".
84: 
85: /// The output of stage 2.3(a): committed hypotheses in the typed vectors plus
86: /// one `SelectedSurface` per committed surface. Regions partition the mesh,
87: /// so every mesh face lands in exactly one selected surface.
88: pub(super) struct Classification {
89:     pub planar: Vec<PlanarHypothesis>,
90:     pub conical: Vec<ConicalHypothesis>,
91:     pub spherical: Vec<SphericalHypothesis>,
92:     pub toroidal: Vec<ToroidalHypothesis>,
93:     pub bspline: Vec<BSplineHypothesis>,
94:     pub selected: Vec<SelectedSurface>,
95: }
96: 
97: impl Classification {
98:     fn new() -> Self {
99:         Classification {
100:             planar: Vec::new(),
101:             conical: Vec::new(),
102:             spherical: Vec::new(),
103:             toroidal: Vec::new(),
104:             bspline: Vec::new(),
105:             selected: Vec::new(),
106:         }
107:     }
108: 
109:     fn push_planar(&mut self, hyp: PlanarHypothesis) {
110:         self.selected.push(SelectedSurface::Planar(self.planar.len()));
111:         self.planar.push(hyp);
112:     }
113:     fn push_conical(&mut self, hyp: ConicalHypothesis) {
114:         self.selected
115:             .push(SelectedSurface::Conical(self.conical.len()));
116:         self.conical.push(hyp);
117:     }
118:     fn push_spherical(&mut self, hyp: SphericalHypothesis) {
119:         self.selected
120:             .push(SelectedSurface::Spherical(self.spherical.len()));
121:         self.spherical.push(hyp);
122:     }
123:     fn push_toroidal(&mut self, hyp: ToroidalHypothesis) {
124:         self.selected
125:             .push(SelectedSurface::Toroidal(self.toroidal.len()));
126:         self.toroidal.push(hyp);
127:     }
128:     fn push_bspline(&mut self, hyp: BSplineHypothesis) {
129:         self.selected
130:             .push(SelectedSurface::BSpline(self.bspline.len()));
131:         self.bspline.push(hyp);
132:     }
133:     fn push_compete(&mut self, hyp: CompeteHyp) {
134:         match hyp {
135:             CompeteHyp::Planar(h) => self.push_planar(h),
136:             CompeteHyp::Conical(h) => self.push_conical(h),
137:             CompeteHyp::Spherical(h) => self.push_spherical(h),
138:             CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h) => self.push_toroidal(h),
139:         }
140:     }
141: }
142: 
143: /// Run the whole-region ladder over every smooth region; regions the ladder
144: /// cannot explain go to scoped competition (2.3(b)), and whatever competition
145: /// leaves unclaimed degrades to per-facet planes.
146: pub(super) fn classify_regions(
147:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
148:     regions: &[Region],
149:     jet: &[JetField],
150:     face_region: &[usize],
151:     config: &Config,
152: ) -> Classification {
153:     let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
154:         .faces
155:         .iter()
156:         .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
157:         .collect();
158: 
159:     let mut cls = Classification::new();
160:     let mut ladder_wins = [0usize; 4]; // plane, sphere, conical, torus
161:     let mut competed_regions = 0usize;
162:     let mut competed_surfaces = 0usize;
163:     let mut freeform_surfaces = 0usize;
164:     let mut facet_faces = 0usize;
165:     for region in regions {
166:         if classify_one_region(mesh, region, &centroids, jet, config, &mut cls, &mut ladder_wins) {
167:             continue;
168:         }
169:         // The ladder cannot explain this region — scoped competition.
170:         competed_regions += 1;
171:         let (mut hyps, leftovers) = compete::compete_region(mesh, region, jet, &centroids, config);
172: 
173:         // Stage 2.3(d): run the whole-region ladder on each connected
174:         // component of the leftovers.
175:         //
176:         // Competition seeds a fit from an unvalidated neighbourhood of one
177:         // face, so a surface whose every seed patch straddles a junction is
178:         // never fitted at all — measured on `stepped_block_toroidal` fine,
179:         // three fillet cylinders are lost this way and reach stage 3 as
180:         // B-splines. But the *component* those faces form is exactly the kind
181:         // of input the ladder handles: given the right face set the existing
182:         // conical fitter estimates its axis from the normal covariance and
183:         // recovers r = 0.99998 / 0.99999 / 0.50008 against true 1.0 / 1.0 /
184:         // 0.5, at five to ten times under vertex tolerance. Nothing new is
185:         // needed — only feeding the fitter a face set that is not
186:         // contaminated by a neighbouring surface.
187:         //
188:         // This can only *add* surfaces: it runs on faces nothing else
189:         // explained, and every rung is vertex-tolerance validated, so a
190:         // component that is genuinely freeform simply falls through to
191:         // 2.3(e) as before.
192:         // Stages 2.3(d) and 2.3(d2), alternated to a fixpoint. Each changes
193:         // the leftover set the other sees: (d2) splitting a blob can leave a
194:         // remainder one ladder rung explains, and (d) committing a surface
195:         // gives (d2) another axis to borrow. Terminating — a round that
196:         // commits nothing ends the loop, and every round strictly shrinks the
197:         // leftovers.
198:         let mut leftovers = leftovers;
199:         loop {
200:             let before = leftovers.len();
201:             leftovers =
202:                 rescue_leftover_components(mesh, &leftovers, &centroids, config, &mut hyps);
203:             leftovers =
204:                 rescue_with_neighbour_axes(mesh, &leftovers, &centroids, config, &mut hyps);
205:             if leftovers.len() == before {
206:                 break;
207:             }
208:         }
209: 
210:         // Stage 2.3(e): freeform-fit the leftover components competition
211:         // could not explain analytically, dissolving any small analytic
212:         // island enclosed by a validating B-spline patch.
213:         let ff = bspline::fit_leftover_components(mesh, &leftovers, &hyps, face_region, config);
214: 
215:         // A committed analytic surface whose boundary with the per-facet
216:         // residual is more fragmented than the surface itself reconstructs to
217:         // an invalid wire; dissolve exactly those, and leave the rest of the
218:         // region's decomposition alone.
219:         let mut residual = ff.residual;
220:         let fragmented = dissolve_fragmented_surfaces(mesh, &hyps, &ff.dissolved, &mut residual);
221:         let surviving_hyps = hyps.len() - ff.dissolved.len() - fragmented.len();
222:         if config.verbosity >= 3 || std::env::var("BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE").is_ok() {
223:             eprintln!(
224:                 "    compete: region {} faces → {surviving_hyps} surviving hyps, {} leftovers, freeform residual {} (largest blob {}, {} dissolved as over-fragmented)",
225:                 region.faces.len(),
226:                 leftovers.len(),
227:                 residual.len(),
228:                 largest_component(mesh, &residual),
229:                 fragmented.len(),
230:             );
231:         }
232: 
233:         // Commit surviving analytic hypotheses (skipping dissolved islands),
234:         // the freeform surfaces, and the residual leftovers as per-facet
235:         // planes (total coverage can never fail; a triangle is exactly a
236:         // plane).
237:         for (i, hyp) in hyps.into_iter().enumerate() {
238:             if !ff.dissolved.contains(&i) && !fragmented.contains(&i) {
239:                 cls.push_compete(hyp);
240:                 competed_surfaces += 1;
241:             }
242:         }
243:         for plane in ff.planes {
244:             cls.push_planar(plane);
245:         }
246:         for hyp in ff.bsplines {
247:             cls.push_bspline(hyp);
248:             freeform_surfaces += 1;
249:         }
250:         facet_faces += residual.len();
251:         for fi in residual {
252:             cls.push_planar(facet_plane(mesh, fi));
253:         }
254:     }
255: 
256:     if config.verbosity >= 1 {
257:         eprintln!(
258:             "  ladder: {} plane, {} sphere, {} cyl/cone, {} torus; competition: {} region(s) → {} analytic + {} freeform + {} per-facet",
259:             ladder_wins[0], ladder_wins[1], ladder_wins[2], ladder_wins[3],
260:             competed_regions, competed_surfaces, freeform_surfaces, facet_faces,
261:         );
262:     }
263:     cls
264: }
265: 
266: /// Median `min(|κ1|,|κ2|)/max(|κ1|,|κ2|)` at or below which a region is treated
267: /// as developable (cylinder/cone) for rung ordering: a cylinder/cone reads ~0
268: /// (one principal curvature vanishes), a sphere reads ~1 (both equal). Well
269: /// clear of both classes' jet noise. Ordering only — never rejection.
270: const DEVELOPABLE_MAX_RATIO: f64 = 0.4;
271: 
272: /// Whether the region's jet reads developable (one principal curvature ~0),
273: /// used only to order the sphere and conical rungs. A short cylinder/cone
274: /// tessellated as a few rings is vertex-ambiguous with a sphere — two
275: /// same-radius rings at heights ±h lie exactly on a sphere of radius
276: /// √(R²+h²) — so simplest-DOF-first would wrongly commit the sphere; the jet
277: /// breaks the tie by curvature class. Faces too flat to have a curvature
278: /// signal are skipped; with no signal at all the region is not treated as
279: /// developable (keeps the plane→sphere→conical default order).
280: fn region_is_developable(region: &Region, jet: &[JetField]) -> bool {
281:     let mut ratios: Vec<f64> = region
282:         .faces
283:         .iter()
284:         .filter_map(|&fi| {
285:             let j = &jet[fi];
286:             let (a, b) = (j.k1.abs(), j.k2.abs());
287:             let (lo, hi) = if a <= b { (a, b) } else { (b, a) };
288:             if hi > 1e-9 {
289:                 Some(lo / hi)
290:             } else {
291:                 None
292:             }
293:         })
294:         .collect();
295:     if ratios.is_empty() {
296:         return false;
297:     }
298:     ratios.sort_by(|a, b| a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap());
299:     ratios[ratios.len() / 2] <= DEVELOPABLE_MAX_RATIO
300: }
301: 
302: /// Try the DOF ladder on one region; commit the first surface that validates.
303: /// Returns true on a committed surface, false if the region fell through
304: /// (caller applies the per-facet fallback).
305: fn classify_one_region(
306:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
307:     region: &Region,
308:     centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
309:     jet: &[JetField],
310:     config: &Config,
311:     cls: &mut Classification,
312:     wins: &mut [usize; 4],
313: ) -> bool {
314:     let faces = &region.faces;
315:     let verts = &region.vertices;
316: 
317:     if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<PlanarSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
318:         cls.push_planar(hyp);
319:         wins[0] += 1;
320:         return true;
321:     }
322: 
323:     let try_sphere = |cls: &mut Classification, wins: &mut [usize; 4]| {
324:         if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<SphericalSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
325:             cls.push_spherical(hyp);
326:             wins[1] += 1;
327:             true
328:         } else {
329:             false
330:         }
331:     };
332:     let try_conical = |cls: &mut Classification, wins: &mut [usize; 4]| {
333:         if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<ConicalSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
334:             cls.push_conical(hyp);
335:             wins[2] += 1;
336:             true
337:         } else {
338:             false
339:         }
340:     };
341: 
342:     // Jet-informed rung order: a developable region tries the conical
343:     // (cylinder/cone) rung before the sphere rung, so a short ring-tessellated
344:     // cylinder is not claimed by an ambiguous equal-radius sphere. The jet
345:     // only orders — a genuine sphere the jet misread as developable still
346:     // falls through to the sphere rung (a cylinder fit cannot validate a
347:     // sphere's vertices).
348:     if region_is_developable(region, jet) {
349:         if try_conical(cls, wins) || try_sphere(cls, wins) {
350:             return true;
351:         }
352:     } else if try_sphere(cls, wins) || try_conical(cls, wins) {
353:         return true;
354:     }
355: 
356:     if let Some(hyp) = fit_validate::<ToroidalSurface>(mesh, faces, verts, centroids, config) {
357:         cls.push_toroidal(hyp);
358:         wins[3] += 1;
359:         return true;
360:     }
361:     false
362: }
363: 
364: /// Fit surface type `S` to a region and validate it against the whole region.
365: /// Large regions fit on a stride subsample, refit-augmented with the worst
366: /// validation offenders on a near-miss. Returns the committed hypothesis, or
367: /// `None` if `S` cannot explain the region within tolerance.
368: ///
369: /// Also the stage-2.4 merge revalidation entry point (`repair.rs`): refit a
370: /// candidate union and confirm it validates end to end before committing.
371: pub(super) fn fit_validate<S: SurfaceType>(
372:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
373:     region_faces: &[usize],
374:     region_verts: &[usize],
375:     centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
376:     config: &Config,
377: ) -> Option<S::Hypothesis> {
378:     let vtol = config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
379:     let stol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
380: 
381:     // Initial fit set: the whole region, or a deterministic stride subsample
382:     // when the region is large.
383:     let mut fit_faces: Vec<usize> = if region_faces.len() > MAX_FIT_FACES {
384:         let stride = region_faces.len().div_ceil(MAX_FIT_FACES);
385:         region_faces.iter().copied().step_by(stride).collect()
386:     } else {
387:         region_faces.to_vec()
388:     };
389: 
390:     for _round in 0..=MAX_REFIT {
391:         let fit_verts = vertices_of(mesh, &fit_faces);
392:         let mut fit = S::fit_surface(mesh, &fit_faces, &fit_verts, config.verbosity)?;
393:         S::determine_convexity(mesh, region_faces, &mut fit);
394: 
395:         // Validate every region face; collect near-miss offenders for a refit.
396:         let mut offenders: Vec<(f64, usize)> = Vec::new();
397:         for &fi in region_faces {
398:             // Orientation coherence: a face whose normal disagrees with the
399:             // fitted surface's convexity means the region is not one clean
400:             // surface of type S — no refit rescues that.
401:             if !S::convexity_check(&fit, mesh, fi) {
402:                 return None;
403:             }
404:             let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
405:             let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
406:             let mut excess = 0.0_f64;
407:             for &vi in &face.vertex_indices[..vc] {
408:                 let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
409:                 let d = S::vertex_distance(&fit, &[v.x, v.y, v.z]);
410:                 if d > vtol {
411:                     excess = excess.max(d - vtol);
412:                 }
413:             }
414:             let cd = S::centroid_distance(&fit, &centroids[fi]);
415:             if cd > stol {
416:                 excess = excess.max(cd - stol);
417:             }
418:             if excess > 0.0 {
419:                 offenders.push((excess, fi));
420:             }
421:         }
422: 
423:         if offenders.is_empty() {
424:             return Some(S::make_hypothesis(
425:                 mesh,
426:                 region_faces.to_vec(),
427:                 region_verts.to_vec(),
428:                 &fit,
429:             ));
430:         }
431: 
432:         // Near-miss handling: if the worst offender is far out, the region is
433:         // decisively not this surface. Otherwise fold the worst offenders
434:         // into the fit set and refit (bounded rounds).
435:         offenders.sort_by(|a, b| {
436:             b.0.partial_cmp(&a.0)
437:                 .unwrap()
438:                 .then_with(|| a.1.cmp(&b.1))
439:         });
440:         if offenders[0].0 > REFIT_GIVE_UP * stol {
441:             return None;
442:         }
443:         let before = fit_faces.len();
444:         let existing: HashSet<usize> = fit_faces.iter().copied().collect();
445:         for &(_, fi) in offenders.iter().take(REFIT_ADD) {
446:             if !existing.contains(&fi) {
447:                 fit_faces.push(fi);
448:             }
449:         }
450:         fit_faces.sort_unstable();
451:         fit_faces.dedup();
452:         if fit_faces.len() == before {
453:             // No new faces to learn from — cannot improve.
454:             return None;
455:         }
456:     }
457:     None
458: }
459: 
460: /// Size of the largest mesh-connected component within a face set.
461: /// Try the whole-region DOF ladder on each connected component of the
462: /// leftovers, appending any that validate to `hyps` and returning the faces
463: /// still unexplained.
464: ///
465: /// Rungs in ascending DOF, first validating fit wins, exactly as
466: /// `classify_one_region` does — a leftover component *is* a region that
467: /// competition failed to explain, so it deserves the same treatment before
468: /// freeform gets it.
469: fn rescue_leftover_components(
470:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
471:     leftovers: &[usize],
472:     centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
473:     config: &Config,
474:     hyps: &mut Vec<CompeteHyp>,
475: ) -> Vec<usize> {
476:     let mut still: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
477:     for comp in components_of(mesh, leftovers) {
478:         if comp.len() < MIN_CYLINDER_FACES {
479:             still.extend(comp);
480:             continue;
481:         }
482:         let verts = vertices_of(mesh, &comp);
483:         if let Some(h) = fit_validate::<PlanarSurface>(mesh, &comp, &verts, centroids, config) {
484:             hyps.push(CompeteHyp::Planar(h));
485:         } else if let Some(h) = (comp.len() >= MIN_SPHERE_FACES)
486:             .then(|| fit_validate::<SphericalSurface>(mesh, &comp, &verts, centroids, config))
487:             .flatten()
488:         {
489:             hyps.push(CompeteHyp::Spherical(h));
490:         } else if let Some(h) =
491:             fit_validate::<ConicalSurface>(mesh, &comp, &verts, centroids, config)
492:         {
493:             hyps.push(CompeteHyp::Conical(h));
494:         } else if let Some(h) = (comp.len() >= MIN_TORUS_FACES)
495:             .then(|| fit_validate::<ToroidalSurface>(mesh, &comp, &verts, centroids, config))
496:             .flatten()
497:         {
498:             hyps.push(CompeteHyp::Toroidal(h));
499:         } else {
500:             still.extend(comp);
501:         }
502:     }
503:     still.sort_unstable();
504:     still
505: }
506: 
507: /// Grow cylinders through the remaining leftovers using axis directions
508: /// borrowed from adjacent committed surfaces, largest validated area first.
509: fn rescue_with_neighbour_axes(
510:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
511:     leftovers: &[usize],
512:     centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
513:     config: &Config,
514:     hyps: &mut Vec<CompeteHyp>,
515: ) -> Vec<usize> {
516:     let mut open: HashSet<usize> = leftovers.iter().copied().collect();
517:     // face -> committed hypothesis index, for finding a component's neighbours
518:     let mut owner: Vec<usize> = vec![usize::MAX; mesh.faces.len()];
519:     for (hi, h) in hyps.iter().enumerate() {
520:         for &fi in h.faces() {
521:             owner[fi] = hi;
522:         }
523:     }
524: 
525:     loop {
526:         // Candidate axes: the axis directions of every committed surface
527:         // adjacent to something still open.
528:         let mut axes: Vec<[f64; 3]> = Vec::new();
529:         for &fi in open.iter() {
530:             let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
531:             for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
532:                 if ni < 0 {
533:                     continue;
534:                 }
535:                 let hi = owner[ni as usize];
536:                 if hi == usize::MAX {
537:                     continue;
538:                 }
539:                 let ax = match &hyps[hi] {
540:                     CompeteHyp::Conical(c) => c.axis_direction,
541:                     CompeteHyp::Toroidal(t) => t.axis_direction,
542:                     _ => continue,
543:                 };
544:                 if !axes.iter().any(|a| {
545:                     let d = a[0] * ax[0] + a[1] * ax[1] + a[2] * ax[2];
546:                     d.abs() > 0.999_999
547:                 }) {
548:                     axes.push(ax);
549:                 }
550:             }
551:         }
552:         if axes.is_empty() {
553:             break;
554:         }
555: 
556:         // The arena for this round is what remains open.
557:         let mut in_open = vec![false; mesh.faces.len()];
558:         for &fi in open.iter() {
559:             in_open[fi] = true;
560:         }
561:         let mut seeds: Vec<usize> = open.iter().copied().collect();
562:         seeds.sort_unstable();
563: 
564:         let mut best: Option<(ConicalHypothesis, f64, Vec<usize>)> = None;
565:         for &seed in &seeds {
566:             for &axis in &axes {
567:                 if let Some((h, area, faces)) =
568:                     compete::grow_with_axis(mesh, &in_open, seed, axis, centroids, config)
569:                 {
570:                     if best.as_ref().is_none_or(|(_, ba, _)| area > *ba + 1e-12) {
571:                         best = Some((h, area, faces));
572:                     }
573:                 }
574:             }
575:         }
576: 
577:         match best {
578:             Some((h, _, faces)) => {
579:                 let hi = hyps.len();
580:                 for &fi in &faces {
581:                     open.remove(&fi);
582:                     owner[fi] = hi;
583:                 }
584:                 hyps.push(CompeteHyp::Conical(h));
585:             }
586:             None => break,
587:         }
588:     }
589: 
590:     let mut still: Vec<usize> = open.into_iter().collect();
591:     still.sort_unstable();
592:     still
593: }
594: 
595: /// Connected components of a face set, over mesh adjacency.
596: fn components_of(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<Vec<usize>> {
597:     let member: HashSet<usize> = faces.iter().copied().collect();
598:     let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
599:     let mut out: Vec<Vec<usize>> = Vec::new();
600:     let mut starts = faces.to_vec();
601:     starts.sort_unstable();
602:     for &start in &starts {
603:         if seen.contains(&start) {
604:             continue;
605:         }
606:         let mut comp = Vec::new();
607:         let mut stack = vec![start];
608:         seen.insert(start);
609:         while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
610:             comp.push(fi);
611:             let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
612:             for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
613:                 if ni >= 0 {
614:                     let ni = ni as usize;
615:                     if member.contains(&ni) && seen.insert(ni) {
616:                         stack.push(ni);
617:                     }
618:                 }
619:             }
620:         }
621:         comp.sort_unstable();
622:         out.push(comp);
623:     }
624:     out
625: }
626: 
627: /// Dissolve into the per-facet residual any committed surface whose boundary
628: /// with that residual is more fragmented than the surface itself, and return
629: /// the indices dissolved.
630: ///
631: /// This replaces the old whole-region discard, which threw away a region's
632: /// entire decomposition whenever freeform left a connected leftover blob
633: /// bigger than a fixed size. The premise it protected is real — a surface
634: /// abutting a jagged per-facet blob reconstructs to an invalid wire — but the
635: /// remedy was wildly out of proportion to it, and *size was the wrong
636: /// quantity*. Measured: `cone_tangent_planes` leaves a blob covering 73% of
637: /// its region and reconstructs with zero BRepCheck failures, while
638: /// `bspline_blister` leaves one covering 43% and produces sixteen invalid
639: /// faces — so no threshold on the blob, absolute or relative, separates them.
640: ///
641: /// What does separate them is *where the invalidity comes from*: a face's wire
642: /// gains one edge per distinct per-facet neighbour, so the face that goes
643: /// invalid is the one whose boundary has been shattered into more pieces than
644: /// the face has substance. Comparing a surface's per-facet neighbour count
645: /// against its own face count says exactly that, carries no length and no
646: /// tuned number, and dissolves only the surfaces that are actually in trouble
647: /// rather than every surface that happens to share a region with them.
648: ///
649: /// Iterated to a fixpoint, because dissolving one surface enlarges the
650: /// residual and can shatter its neighbour's boundary in turn. In the limit
651: /// this reproduces the old whole-region behaviour — but only when every
652: /// surface in the region individually earns it.
653: fn dissolve_fragmented_surfaces(
654:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
655:     hyps: &[CompeteHyp],
656:     already_dissolved: &HashSet<usize>,
657:     residual: &mut Vec<usize>,
658: ) -> HashSet<usize> {
659:     let mut dissolved: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
660:     let mut in_residual: HashSet<usize> = residual.iter().copied().collect();
661:     loop {
662:         let mut round: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
663:         for (i, hyp) in hyps.iter().enumerate() {
664:             if already_dissolved.contains(&i) || dissolved.contains(&i) {
665:                 continue;
666:             }
667:             let own: HashSet<usize> = hyp.faces().iter().copied().collect();
668:             let mut neighbours: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
669:             for &fi in hyp.faces() {
670:                 let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
671:                 for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
672:                     if ni >= 0 {
673:                         let ni = ni as usize;
674:                         if in_residual.contains(&ni) && !own.contains(&ni) {
675:                             neighbours.insert(ni);
676:                         }
677:                     }
678:                 }
679:             }
680:             if neighbours.len() > hyp.faces().len() {
681:                 round.push(i);
682:             }
683:         }
684:         if round.is_empty() {
685:             return dissolved;
686:         }
687:         for i in round {
688:             dissolved.insert(i);
689:             for &fi in hyps[i].faces() {
690:                 if in_residual.insert(fi) {
691:                     residual.push(fi);
692:                 }
693:             }
694:         }
695:     }
696: }
697: 
698: fn largest_component(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> usize {
699:     let member: HashSet<usize> = faces.iter().copied().collect();
700:     let mut seen: HashSet<usize> = HashSet::new();
701:     let mut largest = 0usize;
702:     for &start in faces {
703:         if seen.contains(&start) {
704:             continue;
705:         }
706:         let mut size = 0usize;
707:         let mut stack = vec![start];
708:         seen.insert(start);
709:         while let Some(fi) = stack.pop() {
710:             size += 1;
711:             let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
712:             for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
713:                 if ni >= 0 {
714:                     let ni = ni as usize;
715:                     if member.contains(&ni) && seen.insert(ni) {
716:                         stack.push(ni);
717:                     }
718:                 }
719:             }
720:         }
721:         largest = largest.max(size);
722:     }
723:     largest
724: }
725: 
726: /// Sorted, deduplicated vertex indices of a face set.
727: fn vertices_of(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, faces: &[usize]) -> Vec<usize> {
728:     let mut v: Vec<usize> = faces
729:         .iter()
730:         .flat_map(|&fi| {
731:             let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
732:             mesh.faces[fi].vertex_indices[..vc].iter().copied()
733:         })
734:         .collect();
735:     v.sort_unstable();
736:     v.dedup();
737:     v
738: }
739: 
740: /// A single-face planar hypothesis (the terminal per-facet fallback).
741: fn facet_plane(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> PlanarHypothesis {
742:     let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
743:     let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
744:     let vertices: Vec<usize> = face.vertex_indices[..vc].to_vec();
745:     let vertex_set: HashSet<usize> = vertices.iter().copied().collect();
746:     let (normal, distance) = fit_plane(&[fi], &vertex_set, &mesh.faces, &mesh.vertices);
747:     let mut error_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
748:     let mut error_min = f64::INFINITY;
749:     let mut error_abs_sum = 0.0_f64;
750:     for &vi in &vertices {
751:         let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
752:         let d = normal[0] * v.x + normal[1] * v.y + normal[2] * v.z - distance;
753:         error_max = error_max.max(d);
754:         error_min = error_min.min(d);
755:         error_abs_sum += d.abs();
756:     }
757:     PlanarHypothesis {
758:         normal,
759:         distance,
760:         faces: vec![fi],
761:         vertices,
762:         error_max,
763:         error_min,
764:         error_abs_sum,
765:     }
766: }

<content>
1: //! Stage 2.2: region extraction — connected components of the face graph
2: //! over INTERIOR edges (union-find). Regions are maximal, mesh-connected by
3: //! construction, and every face belongs to exactly one region (a single
4: //! facet is a valid region — the terminal per-facet-plane fallback of the
5: //! classification ladder depends on that). Region numbering is
6: //! deterministic: regions are ordered by their smallest member face index.
7: 
8: use super::edges::EdgeClassification;
9: use crate::stage1::ConnectedMesh;
10: 
11: /// One region of the partition: a maximal set of faces connected across
12: /// INTERIOR edges.
13: #[derive(Debug, Default)]
14: pub struct Region {
15:     /// Member mesh face indices, ascending.
16:     pub faces: Vec<usize>,
17:     /// Member mesh vertex indices (union of face vertices), sorted, deduped.
18:     pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
19: }
20: 
21: fn find(parent: &mut [usize], mut x: usize) -> usize {
22:     while parent[x] != x {
23:         parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]; // path halving
24:         x = parent[x];
25:     }
26:     x
27: }
28: 
29: /// Extract regions from the edge classification. Returns
30: /// `(face_region, regions)` where `face_region[fi]` is the region id of
31: /// face `fi`.
32: pub fn extract_regions(
33:     mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
34:     edge_classes: &EdgeClassification,
35: ) -> (Vec<usize>, Vec<Region>) {
36:     let n = mesh.faces.len();
37:     let mut parent: Vec<usize> = (0..n).collect();
38: 
39:     for fi in 0..n {
40:         let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
41:         for e in 0..vc {
42:             let ni = mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[e];
43:             if ni >= 0 && !edge_classes.is_boundary(fi, e) {
44:                 let ra = find(&mut parent, fi);
45:                 let rb = find(&mut parent, ni as usize);
46:                 if ra != rb {
47:                     // Union by smaller root index keeps numbering stable.
48:                     parent[ra.max(rb)] = ra.min(rb);
49:                 }
50:             }
51:         }
52:     }
53: 
54:     let mut face_region = vec![usize::MAX; n];
55:     let mut region_of_root = vec![usize::MAX; n];
56:     let mut regions: Vec<Region> = Vec::new();
57:     for (fi, slot) in face_region.iter_mut().enumerate() {
58:         let root = find(&mut parent, fi);
59:         let id = if region_of_root[root] == usize::MAX {
60:             regions.push(Region::default());
61:             region_of_root[root] = regions.len() - 1;
62:             regions.len() - 1
63:         } else {
64:             region_of_root[root]
65:         };
66:         *slot = id;
67:         regions[id].faces.push(fi);
68:     }
69: 
70:     for region in &mut regions {
71:         let mut vertices: Vec<usize> = region
72:             .faces
73:             .iter()
74:             .flat_map(|&fi| {
75:                 let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
76:                 mesh.faces[fi].vertex_indices[..vc].iter().copied()
77:             })
78:             .collect();
79:         vertices.sort_unstable();
80:         vertices.dedup();
81:         region.vertices = vertices;
82:     }
83: 
84:     (face_region, regions)
85: }

877: fn fit_cone_primary(
878:     face_indices: &[usize],
879:     vertex_set: &[usize],
880:     faces: &[MeshFace],
881:     vertices: &[MeshVertex],
882: ) -> Option<([f64; 3], [f64; 3], f64)> {
883:     if vertex_set.len() < 4 {
884:         return None;
885:     }
886: 
887:     // Check for pointed cone: a vertex present in ALL seed faces.
888:     let shared_apex = find_shared_apex_vertex(face_indices, faces, vertices);
889: 
890:     // Recenter vertices around their centroid for numerical stability.
891:     let n_pts = vertex_set.len() as f64;
892:     let mut centroid = [0.0_f64; 3];
893:     for &vi in vertex_set.iter() {
894:         centroid[0] += vertices[vi].x;
895:         centroid[1] += vertices[vi].y;
896:         centroid[2] += vertices[vi].z;
897:     }
898:     centroid[0] /= n_pts;
899:     centroid[1] /= n_pts;
900:     centroid[2] /= n_pts;
901: 
902:     let centered_points: Vec<[f64; 3]> = vertex_set.iter()
903:         .map(|&vi| [
904:             vertices[vi].x - centroid[0],
905:             vertices[vi].y - centroid[1],
906:             vertices[vi].z - centroid[2],
907:         ])
908:         .collect();
909: 
910:     if let Some(apex) = shared_apex {
911:         // --- Pointed cone path ---
912:         // For fixed-apex LM, recenter both points and apex.
913:         let centered_apex = [
914:             apex[0] - centroid[0],
915:             apex[1] - centroid[1],
916:             apex[2] - centroid[2],
917:         ];
918:         let mut axis = estimate_axis_from_normal_diffs(face_indices, faces)
919:             .or_else(|| estimate_axis_from_apex(&apex, vertex_set, vertices))?;
920:         let half_angle = estimate_half_angle(&apex, &mut axis, vertex_set, vertices)?;

922:         let problem = ConeFixedApexLMProblem::new(centered_points, centered_apex, axis, half_angle);

924:         let refined_dir = result.axis_dir_from_params(&result.params);
925:         let refined_ha = result.params[2];

927:             Some((apex, refined_dir, refined_ha))
928:         } else {
929:             Some((apex, axis, half_angle))
930:         }
931:     } else {
932:         // --- Truncated cone path ---
933:         // Multi-strategy axis: covariance for ≥8 faces, vertex-based for seeds
934:         let mut axis = compute_truncated_cone_axis(
935:             face_indices, vertex_set, faces, vertices)?;
936: 
937:         // Step 2+3: Apex and half-angle from linear regression of vertex projections.
938:         let (apex, half_angle) = estimate_apex_and_angle_from_profile(
939:             &mut axis, vertex_set, vertices)?;
940: 
941:         // Full LM refinement with recentered coordinates
942:         let centered_apex = [
943:             apex[0] - centroid[0],
944:             apex[1] - centroid[1],

950:         let refined_apex = [
951:             result.params[2] + centroid[0],
952:             result.params[3] + centroid[1],
953:             result.params[4] + centroid[2],
954:         ];
955:         let refined_ha = result.params[5];

967: /// Find a vertex that appears in all faces (the apex of a pointed cone).
968: fn find_shared_apex_vertex(
969:     face_indices: &[usize],
970:     faces: &[MeshFace],
971:     vertices: &[MeshVertex],
972: ) -> Option<[f64; 3]> {
973:     let n_faces = face_indices.len();
974:     if n_faces < 3 {
975:         return None;
976:     }
977:     let mut vertex_face_count = std::collections::HashMap::<usize, usize>::new();
978:     for &fi in face_indices {
979:         let vc = faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
980:         for &vi in &faces[fi].vertex_indices[..vc] {
981:             *vertex_face_count.entry(vi).or_insert(0) += 1;
982:         }
983:     }
984:     for (&vi, &count) in &vertex_face_count {
985:         if count == n_faces {
986:             let apex = [vertices[vi].x, vertices[vi].y, vertices[vi].z];
987:             // Verify that non-apex vertices are at roughly equal distances from the apex,
988:             // as expected for a cone base. Without this check, any 3 triangles sharing
989:             // a vertex (common in regular triangle meshes) falsely trigger the pointed-cone path.
990:             let mut dists: Vec<f64> = Vec::new();
991:             for &fi in face_indices {
992:                 let vc = faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
993:                 for &vj in &faces[fi].vertex_indices[..vc] {
994:                     if vj != vi {
995:                         let dx = vertices[vj].x - apex[0];
996:                         let dy = vertices[vj].y - apex[1];
997:                         let dz = vertices[vj].z - apex[2];
998:                         dists.push((dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz).sqrt());
999:                     }

**Current architecture summary:**

1. Stage 2.2: regions = connected components over non-sharp edges.
2. Stage 2.3(a): whole-region DOF ladder (`classify_one_region`) — plane → (sphere|conical, jet-ordered) → torus. Uses `fit_validate` which:
   - Subsamples large regions (>3000 faces) with stride
   - Validates against whole region
   - Refit-augment with worst offenders (up to 3 rounds, 32 faces/round)
3. Stage 2.3(b): `compete_region` — for each seed face (every region face seeds once, interior-skip):
   - `seed_patch`: depth-3 BFS capped at 32 faces, jet-similarity filtered with fallback to plain topology
   - `condition_patch`: trim worst-fit faces until seed validates its own patch's fit
   - `grow_from`: fit → flood (BFS with per-face validation at vtol/stol) → refit → reflood to fixpoint (max 8 rounds)
   - Candidates: plane always, cone if curved, sphere if bi-curved, torus if |k2|<0.8|k1| + bent-tube promotion
   - Commit largest validated area per seed; `resolve_claims` at the end.
4. Stage 2.3(d)/(d2): rescue leftover components with the ladder, and borrowed-axis growth.
5. Stage 2.3(e): freeform B-spline fitting of leftovers.

**Fit costs:**
- Plane: closed-form, O(V) — cheap.
- Sphere: need to check but probably closed-form + maybe LM.
- Cylinder: normal-covariance eigenvector + algebraic circle fit + 6-param LM with numeric Jacobian (12 residual evals per Jacobian), patience 150.
- Cone: 6-param LM, patience 150.
- Torus: 7-param LM, patience 150 — most expensive.

The dominant cost per seed: each seed tries up to 4 candidate types, each doing a `grow` = up to 8 rounds of (fit + flood). The flood is BFS over region faces with per-face validation — O(region). The fit involves LM with numeric Jacobians — O(rounds × verts × params × 2).

**The user's proposal:** recursive subdivision (quadtree/octree-like over the mesh graph):
1. For a contiguous face set, fit to a subset of vertices (corners + interior sample).
2. If subset fits → validate with all vertices → if all validate, select.
3. If no fit but plausibly a fit exists within → subdivide and recurse.
4. If clearly no fit → give up.

This is essentially the classic **split-and-merge segmentation** (e.g., the quadtree split in image segmentation; in mesh segmentation it's like the "cluster then fit" approaches). It also resembles RANSAC-family "fit minimal sample, validate on all" but with a deterministic spatial hierarchy instead of random minimal samples.

**Key question: does it have merit here?**

Let me think about what problems it would solve and what problems it wouldn't.

**Where it aligns with the existing machinery:**

1. The whole-region ladder already does the top level of this recursion: fit the whole region (on a stride subsample), validate all vertices, refit-augment with worst offenders. The user's "subset of vertices that includes corners and interior" is what `fit_validate` does with stride subsample + refit augmentation. The difference: when validation fails, the ladder doesn't subdivide — it falls to competition.

2. The competition's flood is a "grow to validation boundary" operation — it already finds the maximal connected validating set for a given fit. In split-and-merge terms, the flood IS the merge step and the validation failure is the split signal. What competition doesn't have is a principled "where to split" — it re-seeds everywhere.

**Where the merit is:**

The expensive part of the current pipeline is the seed sweep: every region face seeds once, each seed tries up to 4 types, each `grow` costs fits (LM) + floods (O(region) per round, up to 8 rounds). On stepped_block_toroidal fine, 26122 faces → ~10K+ non-interior seeds × ~2-4 grows × 8 rounds × (LM fit + flood). That's the 103 s.

A subdivision approach could:
- Drastically reduce the number of *fit attempts*: instead of one attempt per face, one per hierarchy node. For a region of N faces with k surfaces, a balanced recursive split does O(k log(N/k)) node evaluations, each involving a subsampled fit + full validation. Actually validation against all vertices is still O(N) per node, and total validation across the tree is O(N log(N/k)) — comparable to flood costs but without the LM-per-seed.
- Bound the worst case: the tree depth is logarithmic in face count, so you never do worse than O(N log N) validation work, unlike the current per-seed scheme which is O(N) grows each with O(N) floods = O(N²) worst case on a pathological region.

Near-coincident stations (< 0.25× the median arc gap — the near-singular end of the same conditioning problem) are merged, and segments are subdivided to a fixpoint where no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor segment's pieces, uniformly in arc rather than UV (the Floater map compresses high-curvature areas). Surviving boundary-vertex stations keep their exact pinned positions; new substations land only inside long segments, where a long boundary chord means the mesh itself is flat and mid-segment samples lie on the true surface. Genuine hole interiors are filled with a Laplacian membrane so the fitter sees smooth data. The grid is flipped if needed so d1u×d1v matches the mesh's outward normals: B-spline faces are always `concave = false`.
LEARNINGS.md:151:- **The strips are extreme, and the aspect ratio is the ruling announcing itself** (measured on stepped_block_toroidal fine, 2026-07-28): fillet facets are **155:1**, 4.61 mm along the ruling by 0.025 mm across the arc. A faceter subdivides where the surface curves and not where it doesn't, so a developable gets long facets along its zero-curvature direction. Dihedral ÷ spacing across the short direction recovers radius 0.586 mm (true 0.5); along the ruling it is 0 → infinite radius.
LEARNINGS.md:152:- **But do NOT use edge direction as a ruling signal.** On the same model 35% of strip long edges are not axis-parallel, and adjacent strips' long directions differ by up to **76°** — mismatched end subdivisions and Delaunay interiors skew them. It is corroborating at best.
LEARNINGS.md:541:Chord-length-parametrized C2 cubic interpolation is ill-conditioned when adjacent data spacing jumps sharply: noise amplification grows roughly with the SQUARE of the adjacent knot-spacing ratio. On the xyz-cube letter walls (grid stations pinned to boundary vertices mixing 0.175 mm facet chords with one 3.66 mm straight chord — a 21× jump), a ribbon flat to 0.02 mm interpolated to a surface with control points 150 mm off the part — while `IsDone()` returned true, the residual at every grid node was ~1e-14, and per-vertex/per-centroid validation passed (all those sample at or near data points; the excursion lives BETWEEN nodes). Two defenses in stage 2.7: `axis_stations` conditions station spacing in 3D-arc measure (merge stations closer than 0.25× the median gap; subdivide, uniformly in arc, until no piece exceeds 2× either neighbor's pieces), and `fit_grid` rejects any fit with a control point outside the grid data's bounding box inflated by half its diagonal (the surface lies in its poles' convex hull, so poles near the data bound the surface near the data — legit fits, including periodic bands, stay well inside).

/// Absorb small regions into adjacent larger real surfaces (fit-validated).
fn merge_regions(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    cls: &mut Classification,
    centroids: &[[f64; 3]],
    config: &Config,
) -> usize {
    let vtol = config.vertex_tolerance_mm;
    let stol = config.surface_tolerance_mm;
    let mut merged = 0usize;

    loop {
        let f2s = face_to_surface(mesh, cls);
        // Smallest surfaces first (then index) — deterministic.
        let mut order: Vec<usize> = (0..cls.selected.len())
            .filter(|&si| !surface_faces(cls, si).is_empty())
            .collect();
        order.sort_by_key(|&si| (surface_faces(cls, si).len(), si));

        let mut did = false;
        for &si in &order {
            // A B-spline is never a merge source: its fit vouches only for
            // its own data.
            if is_bspline(cls, si) {
                continue;
            }
            let s_faces: Vec<usize> = surface_faces(cls, si).to_vec();
            if s_faces.is_empty() || s_faces.len() > MERGE_MAX_SMALL {
                continue;
            }
            // Adjacent surfaces (real, strictly larger than S).
            let mut neighbors: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
            for &fi in &s_faces {
                let vc = mesh.faces[fi].vertex_count as usize;
                for &ni in &mesh.faces[fi].neighbors[..vc] {
                    if ni >= 0 {
                        let sj = f2s[ni as usize];
                        if sj != usize::MAX && sj != si {
                            neighbors.push(sj);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            neighbors.sort_unstable();
            neighbors.dedup();

            let s_verts = vertices_of(mesh, &s_faces);
            for &ti in &neighbors {
                // A B-spline is never a merge TARGET: a fit vouches only for
                // its data (the 2.3(e) island-dissolution guard is the
                // fit-validated way faces join a freeform patch).
                if is_bspline(cls, ti) {
                    continue;
                }
                let t_len = surface_faces(cls, ti).len();
                if t_len <= 2 || t_len <= s_faces.len() {
                    continue; // T must be a larger, real surface
                }
                // Every S vertex on T within vertex tol, every S centroid
                // within surface tol — "on the same surface" is decisive.
                let verts_ok = s_verts.iter().all(|&vi| {
                    let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
                    point_dist(cls, ti, &[v.x, v.y, v.z]) <= vtol
                });
                if !verts_ok {
                    continue;
                }
                let cents_ok = s_faces.iter().all(|&fi| point_dist(cls, ti, &centroids[fi]) <= stol);
                if !cents_ok {
                    continue;
                }
                // Refit the union and revalidate; keep only if valid.
                let mut union_faces = surface_faces(cls, ti).to_vec();

Key points to make:

**What the idea is, in this codebase's language:**
- The whole-region ladder (`fit_validate`) is already the root of exactly this recursion: fit on a (subsampled) vertex set, validate against ALL vertices/faces, refit-augment on near-miss. The user's "subset including corners + interior, then retry with all vertices" is literally the stride-subsample + full-validation + REFIT_ADD loop in classify.rs:371-458.
- What happens today on ladder failure: fall to `compete_region` = O(N) seed sweep where each seed re-attempts the same "fit small patch → validate by flood" at face granularity. The proposal replaces that fallback with recursion: split the region and re-run the ladder on halves.
- So it's not a foreign technique — it's "make the ladder recursive" instead of "make the ladder fall back to brute-force seeding".

**Merit:**

1. **It deletes the seed concept** — no SEED_PATCH_CAP/MAX_PATCH_DEPTH/PATCH_RADII/PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS/MIN_CONDITION_FACES, no seed ordering (neighbor_jet_mismatch), no interior-skip. That's the step-5 blocker dissolved from a different direction: instead of finding a non-blind bootstrap for per-face seeds, you never seed at all. The blind patch's problem was "a local neighbourhood gives free-fit parameters that may be contaminated". The recursion's answer: the hierarchy finds homogeneous supports by construction; a node that isn't homogeneous fails validation and splits.

2. **Asymptotics genuinely improve.** Today: O(N) seeds × up to 4 candidate grows × up to 8 rounds × (LM fit + O(region) flood). The interior-skip is a heuristic bound on exactly this. Subdivision: depth ⌈log₂(N/floor)⌉; per level, validation is O(N) closed-form distance evals; fits happen once per node on a capped subsample. ~O(N log N) cheap evals + O(k·log) LM fits. For the 26122-face / 71-surface model that's ~10 levels, ~1-2K nodes, vs ~10K+ margin seeds each doing LM fits and region-scale floods. This is the principled version of the TODO item-2 question ("how much of the 26122-face seed sweep does useful work") — answer: only the work along root-to-leaf paths that contain surface boundaries, and the tree does exactly that work.

3. **The user's correctness instinct is right and already validated by the codebase**: vertices lie on the surface to ~1e-6 (TODO B-prime measurements), so fitting on a subsample is sound IF the subsample spans the patch (boundary + interior), and full-vertex validation then decides. `fit_validate` already works exactly this way. Also note: fit-on-subset + validate-on-all is how RANSAC-ish minimal sampling works, but here deterministic.

4. **It subsumes the (d)/(d2) rescues.** Their job is "find a clean support for the fitter". The tree's homogeneous nodes ARE clean supports.

5. **Worst case is bounded** — the depth is logarithmic, and at the floor you get per-facet planes, same terminal fallback as today. For the pure-NURBS region: ladder fails at every node, cost ~2× the nodes, still logarithmically bounded... but actually it never SUCCEEDS so you subdivide all the way to floors: ~2N/floor ladder attempts on tiny patches. Hmm, that's O(N) small fits — same order as today's per-seed scheme on that region, but with a much smaller constant (tiny subsampled fits, no floods). And there's a prune: jet-homogeneous + all-rungs-fail-badly → mark freeform-candidate, stop subdividing, hand to 2.3(e). That prunes the NURBS interior at shallow depth.

**The crux: subdivide vs give up.** My answer: mostly you don't need to distinguish — give up only at (a) the size floor, (b) a jet-homogeneity prune with freeform handoff. Do NOT gate on residual magnitude (project history: non-monotonic responses, overfitting; REFIT_GIVE_UP works only as a give-up on *augmenting*, not as a split decision). Actually wait — REFIT_GIVE_UP already is a "clearly no fit" test that exists and is load-bearing at the ladder level. Hmm, but there it's "no amount of augmenting will help THIS fit", and the response is to fall through to the next rung / competition — not to discard. Under recursion, "fall through" = split. So the answer: the give-up case never discards anything; it either descends or hands to freeform. There's no information-destroying decision to get wrong. That's the elegant part — the user's worry about distinguishing cases dissolves if the two "no fit" outcomes (subdivide vs freeform) are both safe: subdividing a freeform patch wastes bounded time; freeforming an analytic patch is prevented by keeping B-spline strictly LAST (only at nodes where analytic failed AND we choose not to descend — i.e., the prune). The risk asymmetry: subdivide-too-often costs time; freeform-too-early costs correctness (B-spline swallows analytics — the thing TODO warns about: "a B-spline can fit anything"). So the policy: prune to freeform only on strong evidence (jet-homogeneous + analytic failures at multiple consecutive levels?), else descend.

**The wrinkles I found that the user's sketch doesn't cover (the honest additions):**

1. **Split direction: split alone produces fragments; you need the merge half (split-and-merge).** A real surface gets diced across sibling leaves (e.g., a 12-face plane split across two nodes). Leaves that validate the same surface type with compatible parameters must merge — and the merge test already exists in the codebase: fit the UNION fresh and validate it end-to-end (fit_validate on the union, exactly what merge_regions does at region level). Bottom-up merging also re-pins identifiability...

2. **Identifiability inverts.** MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG exists because a small support doesn't pin a revolution radius. Sub-nodes of a real torus legitimately sweep 5° and would fit garbage-R tori that validate locally. The flood-based scheme gets identifiability globally (flood to full extent, then check sweep); the tree gets it from the merge pass (union sweeps more; commit only when identifiable). So per-node guards must become "defer, don't reject": small-sweep fits stay uncommitted merge candidates. This is the main conceptual rework the idea needs — the validation semantics are the same, but "reject" becomes "not yet".

3. **Junction-row ownership doesn't come out of the tree for free.** At a tangent junction, leaves bottom out at per-facet (a leaf straddling the junction validates as nothing but a lone facet = plane). Today's resolve_claims arbitrates junction rows between two committed surfaces (DOF-first, then area). Keep a terminal pass of exactly that kind: leftover junction leaves get absorbed by a neighboring committed surface they validate on (bounded flood over the tiny leftover set), else per-facet plane. The medium-model failure mode ("~14 facets survive as per-facet planes") lives here; item A's locus machinery is the polishing tool for the same spot.

4. **B-spline must stay out of the internal-node ladder** (correctness guard, from TODO's measured dead ends: "the freeform patch validates over these faces is not evidence the region is freeform"). Freeform only at pruned subtrees or the terminal leftover pass (2.3(e) as today).

5. **How to split.** Children must be mesh-contiguous (seam assumption: boundaries must remain edge chains). Cheapest deterministic: double-sweep graph-diameter approximation, two-source BFS to half each. Slightly smarter: use the failed fit's residual structure — cut between the worst offenders and the clean core... but that's the kind of cleverness to add only if measurements demand it. BFS-half is ~free and shape-agnostic. (A BFS-half can produce a lumpy cut that zigzags across a junction; recursion absorbs it — bounded extra depth.)

6. **Ordering within a node: keep the DOF ladder + jet-informed sphere/conical order.** Area-competition (largest-validated-area) is a seed-sweep artifact — it exists because different seeds produce overlapping supports. Nodes are disjoint supports, so simplest-first ladder order suffices, which also structurally prevents the degenerate-super-model problem (a free-R torus never gets to compete with a cylinder that already validated... wait, careful: on a cylinder node, the ladder tries plane (fails), sphere (fails or jet-ordered), conical (validates) → committed, torus never tried. On a torus node, conical fails, torus validates. On a node straddling cylinder+torus: both might fail → split. On a node straddling cylinder+plane: plane fails (cylinder part off), conical fails (plane part off... actually does it? a plane strip plus cylinder — cylinder fit would be pulled; validation fails) → split. Good.

**What it does NOT solve (be honest):**
- The island/model-selection problem (small spurious analytics on freeform regions; the hill_with_hole junk torus; strip-vs-B-spline). A freeform patch subdivides into locally-analytic-looking pieces — arguably subdivision makes MORE such opportunities. The island-absorption machinery (2.3(e)) still needed. BUT: the merge pass helps here in a specific way — spurious micro-fits on a freeform patch have mutually inconsistent parameters, so they don't merge; that doesn't reject them though. This stays the known hard problem, unchanged.
- bspline_blister/saddle: freeform fit failure — orthogonal fitting problem.
- The 4 surplus surfaces on stepped_block fine: that's boundary precision (junction rows), which subdivision alone doesn't nail (point 3 above).
- The NURBS worst case is bounded but not fast (bounded by prune quality).

**Relation to item C (VSA):** subdivision is the divisive dual of VSA's agglomerative Lloyd iteration. Both target "face-level assignment with refit to fixpoint". Subdivision is less of a rewrite: it replaces compete_region's seed loop, keeps the ladder, fit_validate, resolve_claims, the freeform tail — whereas C rewrites 2.3(b)-2.4 wholesale. And the same sweep-harness prerequisite (item 6) applies for measuring before/after. They're not mutually exclusive: the tree could seed VSA's initial proxies. But if subdivision works, it eats much of C's motivation (the parameter deletion happens either way... actually VSA deletes more: flood, resolve_claims...).

**Suggested validation path** (aligns with project methodology):
1. First measure what dominates the 103 s (fits vs floods vs seeds) — one profiling run, so the win is attributable.
2. Build the recursion as a *replacement for the seed sweep only*, keeping: ladder at root, fit_validate, resolve_claims-style terminal absorption, 2.3(d)/(e) tail. Constants deleted vs added tracked (headline metric).

Format: medium-long response with headers. No code changes — analysis only, as asked.

I've read the seeding loop (`compete.rs`), the ladder (`classify.rs`), the `SurfaceType` fitters, the merge machinery, and confirmed nothing like recursive subdivision appears in the refuted-ideas list. Here's my assessment.

## Short verdict: yes, it has real merit — and it's less foreign to the codebase than it sounds

Your idea is best understood as: **make the existing ladder recursive instead of falling back to per-face seeding**. The root of your recursion already exists. `fit_validate` (classify.rs:371) does precisely your steps 1–2: fit on a stride subsample of vertices (capped at 3000 faces), validate against *all* vertices, and on a near-miss fold the worst offenders into the fit set and refit (`REFIT_ADD`/`MAX_REFIT`). What's missing is your step 3: today, when that fails, `classify_regions` gives up and hands the region to `compete_region`, which re-attempts "fit a small patch → validate by flooding" once per face. That's the 103 s. Your proposal replaces that brute-force fallback with recursion.

## One correction to the diagnosis, from the code

"Good seeds require an interior face" is right, but the *why* is largely measured already — it's in the TODO's step-5 notes:

- Seeds aren't single faces today; each grows a blind ≤32-face BFS patch (`seed_patch`). Setting `SEED_PATCH_CAP=1` was measured: stepped_block fine collapses to 24,872 per-facet surfaces. A single facet pins a plane (3 DOF) but not a free 5-DOF cylinder fit — so the blind neighbourhood exists to bootstrap *parameters*, and it fails because its contamination lives in the fit parameters even though the flood re-validates faces.
- The seed *order* is already jet-interior-first (`neighbor_jet_mismatch` ascending), and the interior-skip means that once good surfaces commit, the faces left seeding are *exactly the junction-adjacent margins* — the worst possible seeds, whose patches straddle junctions. That's the "323 NO GROWTH seeds" and much of the runtime.

So the failure isn't that interior seeds are unexplored — it's that the scheme must guess where the interior is, per face, with a blind patch. Your recursion never has to guess: **the hierarchy finds homogeneous supports by construction**. A node straddling a junction fails validation and splits; a node inside one surface validates. That's the step-5 blocker ("a bootstrap that is not a blind neighbourhood") dissolved from a different direction — you never seed at all.

## Where the merit is concrete

1. **Asymptotics.** Today: O(N) seeds × ~4 candidate types × up to 8 grow rounds × (LM fit + O(region) flood). The interior-skip is a heuristic patch on exactly this. The tree: depth ⌈log₂(N/size-floor)⌉ (~10 for the 26K-face model), validation O(N) closed-form distance evals per level, one subsampled fit per node. Rough estimate puts the whole decomposition of stepped_block fine near ~1 s vs 103 s. This is the principled answer to TODO item 2's question ("how much of the seed sweep does useful work"): only nodes on root-to-leaf paths that contain boundaries matter, and the tree visits exactly those.
2. **Constant deletion.** `SEED_PATCH_CAP`, `MAX_PATCH_DEPTH`, `PATCH_RADII`, `PATCH_TRIM_ROUNDS`, `MIN_CONDITION_FACES`, seed ordering, interior-skip — the whole blind-patch family that step 5 wants gone and measured it can't yet delete. Also most of the (d)/(d2) rescue machinery: their entire job is manufacturing a clean support for the fitter, and tree nodes are clean supports.
3. **Simplification of competition semantics.** Largest-validated-area competition exists because seeds produce *overlapping* supports. Tree nodes are disjoint supports, so per-node simplest-DOF-first ladder order suffices — which also structurally prevents the degenerate-super-model problem (a free-R torus never out-areas a cylinder, because on a cylinder node the conical rung commits first and the torus rung is never reached).
4. **Bounded worst case**, as you said: pure-NURBS regions bottom out at per-facet planes (same terminal fallback as today), cost bounded by the tree size.

## Your crux question: subdivide vs. give up

I'd argue the distinction mostly **dissolves** if you set the policy up right. Two observations:

- **Subdividing too often only costs time** (bounded, logarithmic). Committing to freeform too early costs *correctness* — the TODO has measured twice that a B-spline validates happily over genuinely-analytic supports ("the freeform patch validates over these faces is not evidence the region is freeform"). The asymmetry says: default to subdividing; give up only at the size floor, or into the freeform tail.
- **Don't gate the split decision on residual magnitude.** The project's history is littered with non-monotonic residual-threshold responses (the `acceptance_tol` removal, the facet-scaled-growth refutation). `REFIT_GIVE_UP` survives only as a give-up-on-augmenting test, and under recursion its role is just "fall through to the next rung, then split".

So: per node, run the DOF ladder (jet-ordered, as now). If a rung validates → commit. Else split. At the size floor → per-facet plane or the freeform handoff. The one prune worth having for the NURBS worst case is *jet homogeneity*: a node whose curvature field is smooth and generic-bi-curved, where every analytic rung has failed, is a freeform candidate — stop descending and hand the whole subtree to 2.3(e). That's "jet as prior, not gate", consistent with the codebase's philosophy, and its unreliability on coarse meshes costs only time.

## The three wrinkles your sketch doesn't cover (the honest additions)

1. **Split needs its merge half.** Real surfaces get diced across sibling leaves (a 12-face plane spanning two nodes; a fillet strip across five). This is classic split-and-merge: adjacent leaves whose *union* validates as one freshly-fit surface merge, bottom-up. The merge test already exists — it's exactly `merge_regions`' "refit the union and revalidate" (repair.rs:459+). Note this also handles over-splitting within one surface for free.
2. **Identifiability inverts.** Today the flood gives global support first and `MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG` checks it after. In the tree, a sub-node of a real torus legitimately sweeps 5° and fits a garbage-R torus that *validates locally*. So per-node, the sweep guards must become "defer, don't reject": small-sweep fits stay uncommitted merge candidates; identifiability is checked at merge/commit time, when the union's sweep is large. This is the main conceptual rework — validation semantics unchanged, but "reject" becomes "not yet".
3. **Junction-row ownership still needs a terminal pass.** Leaves straddling a tangent junction bottom out at one facet (= a plane), which is the medium-model's "~14 facets survive as per-facet planes" failure mode. Keep `resolve_claims`' logic as a final absorption: leftover leaves join a neighbouring committed surface they validate on (DOF-first preference), else per-facet plane. This is also where item A's locus machinery plugs in for boundary precision — subdivision doesn't fix the 4-surplus-surfaces problem on its own.

And two things to hold onto from the current design: children must be **mesh-contiguous** (the seam assumption — boundaries must remain edge chains; a spatial octree would violate this, a graph bisection preserves it), and the **B-spline stays out of the internal-node ladder** (last resort only, at pruned subtrees or the 2.3(e) tail).

## What it does *not* solve

- The island/model-selection problem (hill_with_hole's junk torus, strip-vs-B-spline). Subdivision arguably creates *more* locally-analytic-looking freeform fragments. Inconsistent-parameter fragments won't merge, which is weak evidence against them, but rejection still needs the model selection the TODO calls genuinely hard. Unchanged.
- `bspline_blister`/`saddle` (the freeform fit itself failing) — orthogonal.
- The NURBS worst case is *bounded*, but fast only if the jet-homogeneity prune fires.

## Relation to item C (VSA)

This is the divisive dual of VSA's agglomerative Lloyd iteration — same "alternate fit/assignment to a fixpoint" core, top-down instead of bottom-up. It's a *smaller* change than C: it replaces `compete_region`'s seed loop and keeps the ladder, `fit_validate`, `resolve_claims`, and the freeform tail intact, whereas C rewrites 2.3(b)–2.4 wholesale. If it works, it delivers a large fraction of C's promised parameter deletion; C could still follow for boundary refinement, or the tree could seed its initial proxies.

## Suggested smallest experiment

Per the project's own methodology: (1) profile the 103 s first (fits vs. floods vs. seed count) so the win is attributable; (2) build the recursion as a drop-in replacement for the seed sweep only — double-sweep-BFS bisection, ladder per node on boundary-inclusive subsamples, union-validate merge, terminal absorption — behind the same probe harness; (3) `tmp/probe.sh` + full suite, with stepped_block **medium** and part_rounded_cube **coarse** as the identifiability stress tests (the step-5 breadcrumbs' own advice). Constant count before/after as the headline metric.

The one-sentence version: you've independently proposed split-and-merge segmentation, and this codebase is unusually well-prepared for it — the ladder, union-revalidation, claim resolution, and the freeform tail are all already built and tested; what's being replaced is only the part (per-face seeding) that every document in the repo already identifies as the problem.

Coplanar fusion merges coplanar ADJACENT pairs — a strip of k coplanar quads would fuse into one long quad? Only pairs, and only if the result is a quad — merging two quads along an edge gives a hexagon unless they're perfectly aligned rectangles. Let me not go down this hole; the point is: incremental growth with per-addition validation will promote at the right time regardless, because validation decides.

4. **The straddle test at promotion boundaries.** Here's the elegant part: with incremental validated growth, the patch NEVER straddles, by construction — each member validated against the evolving fit before admission. The blind-patch contamination problem (fit parameters poisoned) can't happen because every admitted face was within tolerance of the fit computed from already-clean faces. The fit is always computed from validated members only. This directly achieves step 5's goal ("a bootstrap for the free path that is not a blind neighbourhood") — the neighbourhood is no longer blind, it's earned.

Actually a nice property: the grown patch from any seed ON a surface converges to approximately that surface's full extent (or a floodable subset), so different seeds on the same surface produce near-identical hypotheses → resolve_claims sees consistent duplicates rather than competing fragments. And the interior-skip question — seeds in the interior of a committed surface still get skipped. Marginal seeds now produce the right surface instead of NO GROWTH.
